\section{\texorpdfstring{Spectral Geometry, Quadratic Curvature Invariants, McKean--Singer De Rham Supertrace, Jacobi $\vartheta_3/\vartheta_4$ Modular Duality, and Regulator-Independent Jet Basis on $\mathcal{M}_{3,2}$ \catD}{Spectral Geometry, Quadratic Curvature Invariants, McKean-Singer De Rham Supertrace, Jacobi Theta Modular Duality, and Regulator-Independent Jet Basis on M\_\{3,2\}}}
\label{sec:c4_spectral_geometry}

\subsection{Injection of Axiom~4.1 (\texttt{AXIOM\_2}), the Full Riemann Tensor $R^{(5)}_{ABCD}$, Kretschmann Invariant $\mathcal{K}_5$, Extrinsic Boundary Curvature $K_{AB}$, and Radial Throat Holonomy (\texttt{THM~\#16, COR~\#070, \#072})}

\begin{axiom}[{Spectral Action Principle and Orbifold Winding Sector Stipulation --- \texttt{AXIOM\_2} \catA}]\label{ax:axiom_two}
The effective geometric action on $\mathcal{M}_{3,2}$ is generated by the Wick-rotated spectral trace $\mathrm{Tr}\,f(\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{JV}}^2/\Lambda_{\mathrm{cut}}^2)$ of the 5D Dirac--Kostant operator $\widehat{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{JV}} = i\Gamma^M D_M$ (with non-vanishing spectral cutoff moments $f_{4-2k} \neq 0$ for $k \in \{0, 1, 2\}$), where on the orbifold fiber $\mathcal{S}_{t_2} = S^1_{t_2}/\mathbb{Z}_2$ ($\pi_1^{\mathrm{orb}}(\mathcal{S}_{t_2}) = \mathbb{Z}_2 * \mathbb{Z}_2 \simeq D_\infty$) the power-law Mellin winding moments at levels $k \in \{0, 1\}$ are evaluated on the untwisted periodic $\mathbb{Z}_2$-even subbundle $\mathrm{Sym}^2_+$, while at level $k = 2$ ($\Phi^0$) the divergent untwisted harmonic series is discarded and only the convergent antiperiodic Scherk--Schwarz twisted $\mathbb{Z}_2$-odd subbundle $\mathrm{Sym}^2_-$ contributes, yielding finite non-vanishing Mellin winding moments $0 < |\mathcal{M}_k| < \infty$ for all $k \in \{0, 1, 2\}$ whose Weyl-rescaled fiber powers span the three-term potential space $\mathcal{W}_3 = \mathrm{Span}\{e^{-2\psi}, e^{-4\psi}, e^{-6\psi}\}$ \catA.
\end{axiom}

\begin{theorem}[{Exact Block-Decomposition of $R^{(5)}_{ABCD}$, the Quadratic Kretschmann Split $\mathcal{K}_5$ ($c_{\mathcal{K}_5} = 4$), the Extrinsic Trace Identity $K = K^{(4)} + n^\mu\partial_\mu\psi$, and 1D Radial Holonomy vs.\ 4D $S^3$ Flux --- \texttt{THM~\#16, COR~\#070, \#072} \catD}]\label{thm:c4_kretschmann_ghy_identities}
On $\mathcal{M}_{3,2}$ with $\partial_2 g_{\mathrm{proj}} = 0$, the uncontracted Riemann tensor $R^{(5)}_{ABCD}$, the quadratic Kretschmann scalar $\mathcal{K}_5 \equiv R^{(5)}_{ABCD}R^{(5)ABCD}$, and the extrinsic curvature trace $K \equiv g_{\mathrm{proj}}^{AB}K_{AB} = \frac{1}{\sqrt{|g_5|}}\partial_A(\sqrt{|g_5|}\,n^A)$ of a boundary $\partial\mathcal{M}_{3,2} = \partial\mathcal{M}_4 \times_\Phi \mathcal{S}_{t_2}$ satisfy three exact identities:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
  \item \textbf{Uncontracted 5D Riemann Blocks and Non-Diagonal Kretschmann Decomposition ($c_{\mathcal{K}_5} = 4$, \texttt{COR~\#070}):}
  \begin{align}
  R^{(5)}_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta} &= R^{(4)}_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}, \qquad
  R^{(5)}_{\mu 2\nu 2} = \Phi\,{}^{(4)}\nabla_\nu\partial_\mu\Phi, \qquad
  R^{(5)}_{\mu\nu\alpha 2} \equiv 0 \;\;\catD, \label{eq:c4_riemann_blocks} \\[4pt]
  \mathcal{K}_5 &\equiv R^{(5)}_{ABCD}R^{(5)ABCD} = \mathcal{K}_4 + \frac{c_{\mathcal{K}_5}}{\Phi^2}\left({}^{(4)}\nabla_\mu\partial_\nu\Phi\right)\!\left({}^{(4)}\nabla^\mu\partial^\nu\Phi\right) \qquad (c_{\mathcal{K}_5} = 4) \;\;\catD. \label{eq:c4_kretschmann_split}
  \end{align}
  \item \textbf{Extrinsic Trace Split ($K = K^{(4)} + n^\mu\partial_\mu\psi$) on Non-Diagonal Metrics and Dirichlet Boundary Cancellation (\texttt{COR~\#072}):}
  For any hypersurface $r = \mathrm{const}$ with unit normal $n^A = g_{\mathrm{proj}}^{Ar}/\sqrt{g_{\mathrm{proj}}^{rr}} = (n^\mu, 0)$ ($g_{AB}^{\mathrm{proj}}n^A n^B = g_{\mu\nu}n^\mu n^\nu = 1$, $\sqrt{|h_4|} = \Phi\sqrt{|h_3|}$), the covariant divergence $K = \nabla_A n^A$ and the Gibbons--Hawking--York integral $\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{GHY}} \equiv \frac{2\epsilon_n}{\pi L_0}\oint_{\partial\mathcal{M}_{3,2}} d^4\Sigma\sqrt{|h_4|}\,K$ satisfy:
  \begin{align}
  K &= \frac{1}{\Phi\sqrt{-g_4}}\partial_\mu\left(\Phi\sqrt{-g_4}\,n^\mu\right) = K^{(4)} + \frac{1}{\Phi}n^\mu\partial_\mu\Phi = K^{(4)} + n^\mu\partial_\mu\psi \;\;\catD, \label{eq:c4_extrinsic_trace_split} \\[4pt]
  \mathcal{I}_5 + \mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{GHY}} &= \int_{\mathcal{M}_4} d^4x\sqrt{-g_4}\,\Phi\,R^{(4)} + 2\oint_{\partial\mathcal{M}_4} d^3\Sigma\sqrt{|h_3|}\,\Phi\,K^{(4)} \qquad (\epsilon_n = +1) \;\;\catD, \label{eq:c4_ghy_cancellation}
  \end{align}
  cancelling the boundary divergence $-2\oint d^3\Sigma\sqrt{|h_3|}\,n^\mu\partial_\mu\Phi$ of Eq.~\eqref{eq:c3_i5_4d_split} identically.
  \item \textbf{1D Radial Logarithmic Holonomy ($\Delta\ell_\partial^{(n)} = 2n\ln\Phi_\infty \implies \Phi_\infty^{2n}$) and 4D $S^3_\rho$ Surface Flux (\texttt{COR~\#072}):}
  Along a 1D radial throat generator $\rho \in [\rho_{\min}, \rho_{\max}]$ connecting a unit-normalized core boundary ($\Phi_{\mathrm{core}} = 1$, $\psi_{\mathrm{core}} = -\ln\Phi_\infty$) to the asymptotic reference scale ($\Phi_{\mathrm{vac}} = \Phi_\infty$, $\psi_{\mathrm{vac}} = 0$), integrating twice the extrinsic trace difference $2(K - K^{(4)})\,d\rho = 2\,n^\mu\partial_\mu(\ln\Phi)\,d\rho = 2\,d(\ln\Phi)$ across $n \in \mathbb{Z}_+$ reflective bounces in the dihedral orbifold group $\pi_1^{\mathrm{orb}}(\mathcal{S}_{t_2}) = \mathbb{Z}_2 * \mathbb{Z}_2 \simeq D_\infty$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:c1_rz2_projection}(iii)) yields the exact 1D radial holonomy and exponential holonomy factor:
  \begin{equation}\label{eq:c4_radial_throat_holonomy}
  \Delta\ell_\partial^{(n)} \equiv 2n\int_{\rho_{\min}}^{\rho_{\max}}\frac{d\psi}{d\rho}\,d\rho = 2n\int_{\Phi=1}^{\Phi=\Phi_\infty}\frac{d\Phi}{\Phi} = 2n\ln\Phi_\infty \;\;\Longrightarrow\;\; \exp\!\left(\Delta\ell_\partial^{(n)}\right) = \Phi_\infty^{2n} \;\;\catD,
  \end{equation}
  while the 4D $O(4)$-symmetric GHY surface flux integrated over $S^3_\rho$ ($\mathrm{Vol}(S^3_1) = 2\pi^2$) evaluates to $\Delta\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{GHY}}^{(S^3)} = 2\oint_{S^3_\rho}\rho^3\psi'(\rho)\,d\Omega_3 = 4\pi^2 C_{\mathrm{throat}}$ (Section~\ref{sec:c5_symmetry_reductions}, \texttt{COR~\#091}) \catD.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Summing the four equivalent index permutations $(\mu, 2, \nu, 2)$, $(2, \mu, 2, \nu)$, $(\mu, 2, 2, \nu)$, and $(2, \mu, \nu, 2)$ with $(g_{\mathrm{proj}}^{22})^2 = \Phi^{-4}$ in $R^{(5)}_{ABCD}R^{(5)ABCD}$ yields Eq.~\eqref{eq:c4_kretschmann_split}. Expanding $\frac{1}{\Phi\sqrt{-g_4}}\partial_\mu(\Phi\sqrt{-g_4}\,n^\mu)$ for the unit normal $n^\mu = g^{\mu r}/\sqrt{g^{rr}}$ yields Eq.~\eqref{eq:c4_extrinsic_trace_split}, whose 1D line integral from $\Phi = 1$ to $\Phi = \Phi_\infty$ proves Eq.~\eqref{eq:c4_radial_throat_holonomy}.
\end{proof}

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{The Orbifold Heat-Kernel Trace $\Theta_{\mathcal{S}_{t_2}}(s; \Phi)$, Jacobi $\vartheta_3$ Inversion, McKean--Singer De Rham Supertrace $\mathrm{Str}_{\Omega^\bullet}(e^{-s\Delta}) = +1$, and Boundary Defects $(\pm\frac{1}{2})$ (\texttt{THM~\#17 \& \#19, COR~\#073, \#078--\#079})}{The Orbifold Heat-Kernel Trace, Jacobi Theta Inversion, McKean-Singer De Rham Supertrace, and Boundary Defects}}

\begin{theorem}[Exact Jacobi $\vartheta_3$ Inversion, McKean--Singer De Rham Cohomological Supertrace $\mathrm{Str}_{\Omega^\bullet}(e^{-s\Delta}) = \chi(\mathcal{S}_{t_2}) = +1$ ($b_0=1, b_1=0$), Deduced Mirror-Boundary Defects $(+\frac{1}{2}, -\frac{1}{2})$, Weyl's Volume Law, and Winding Remainder --- \texttt{THM~\#17 \& \#19, COR~\#073, \#078--\#079} \catD]\label{thm:c4_jacobi_poisson_weyl}\label{thm:c4_orbifold_heat_trace}
For $s > 0$, the untwisted ($\mathbb{Z}_2$-even Neumann 0-form) spectral heat trace $\Theta_{\mathcal{S}_{t_2}}(s; \Phi) \equiv \Theta_{\Omega^0}^{(N)}(s; \Phi) = \sum_{n=0}^{\infty} \exp\!\big(-s\frac{n^2}{L_0^2\Phi^2}\big)$ and the Dirichlet 1-form heat trace $\Theta_{\Omega^1}^{(D)}(s; \Phi) = \sum_{n=1}^{\infty} \exp\!\big(-s\frac{n^2}{L_0^2\Phi^2}\big)$ of $\Delta = -\frac{1}{\Phi^2}\partial_2^2$ on $\mathcal{S}_{t_2} = S^1_{t_2}/\mathbb{Z}_2 = [0, \pi L_0]$ satisfy:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
  \item \textbf{50-Digit Poisson--Jacobi $\vartheta_3$ Inversion and Winding Remainder (\texttt{COR~\#073}):}
  \begin{align}
  \Theta_{\mathcal{S}_{t_2}}(s; \Phi) &= \frac{1}{2}\,\vartheta_3\!\left(0\,\middle|\,\frac{is}{\pi L_0^2\,\Phi^2}\right) + \frac{1}{2} \nonumber \\[3pt]
  &= \frac{\pi L_0\,\Phi}{\sqrt{4\pi s}} + \frac{1}{2} + \widetilde{\Theta}_{\mathrm{wind}}(s; \Phi) \;\;\catD, \label{eq:c4_poisson_winding_sum} \\[4pt]
  \widetilde{\Theta}_{\mathrm{wind}}(s; \Phi) &\equiv \frac{\sqrt{\pi}\,L_0\,\Phi}{\sqrt{s}}\sum_{w=1}^{\infty} \exp\!\left(-\frac{\pi^2 w^2 L_0^2\,\Phi^2}{s}\right) \;\;\catD, \label{eq:c4_winding_remainder_def}
  \end{align}
  where $\frac{\pi L_0\Phi}{\sqrt{4\pi s}} = \frac{\mathrm{Vol}_1(\mathcal{S}_{t_2})}{\sqrt{4\pi s}} \equiv W_{\mathrm{Weyl}}(s)$ is Weyl's volume term ($w = 0$ of the Poisson-resummed kernel) and the sum over $w \ge 1$ indexes the reflective dihedral image geodesics in $\pi_1^{\mathrm{orb}}(\mathcal{S}_{t_2}) \simeq D_\infty$.
  \item \textbf{McKean--Singer De Rham Cohomological Supertrace $\mathrm{Str}_{\Omega^\bullet}(e^{-s\Delta}) = \chi(\mathcal{S}_{t_2}) = +1$ and Deduced Boundary Defects $(+\frac{1}{2}, -\frac{1}{2})$ (\texttt{COR~\#078--\#079}):}
  Imposing $\mathbb{Z}_2$-invariance ($f(-y) = +f(y)$ for 0-forms and $g(-y) = -g(y)$ for 1-forms $g(y)\,dy$) together with $2\pi L_0$-periodicity on the covering circle $S^1_{t_2}$ for $-f''(y) = \mu^2 f(y)$ yields secular $2\times 2$ minors proportional to $\sin(\pi L_0\mu)$ (quantizing the excited spectrum at $\mu_n = n/L_0$ with multiplicity 1 for both 0-forms and 1-forms, paired isospectrally by $d\cos(n x^2/L_0) = -(n/L_0)\sin(n x^2/L_0)\,dx^2$), while at $\mu = 0$ ($-f'' = 0$) the constraint matrices have ranks $(1, 2)$ on $\mathbb{R}^2$, proving $b_0 = \dim H^0_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathcal{S}_{t_2}) = 2 - 1 = 1$ and $b_1 = \dim H^1_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathcal{S}_{t_2}) = 2 - 2 = 0$. Consequently, the De Rham supertrace is $s$-independent and equal to the Euler characteristic $\chi([0, \pi L_0]) = b_0 - b_1 = +1$, which combined with Poisson--Jacobi inversion $1 + 2\sum_{n=1}^\infty e^{-s\lambda_n} = 2 W_{\mathrm{Weyl}}(s)(1 + 2 S_{\mathrm{wind}}(s))$ deductively fixes the constant boundary defects $a_{1/2}^{(N)} = +\frac{1}{2}$ and $a_{1/2}^{(D)} = -\frac{1}{2}$, the boundary cardinality $|\partial\mathcal{S}_{t_2}| = 2$, and the UV $s^{-1/2}$ Weyl cancellation against the Scherk--Schwarz twisted trace $\Theta_{\mathrm{SS}}^{(-)}(s)$:
  \begin{align}
  \mathrm{Str}_{\Omega^\bullet}\!\left(e^{-s\Delta}\right) &\equiv \Theta_{\Omega^0}^{(N)}(s; \Phi) - \Theta_{\Omega^1}^{(D)}(s; \Phi)
  = b_0 - b_1 = 1 - 0 = +1 = \chi(\mathcal{S}_{t_2})
  \quad \textnormal{(\texttt{COR~\#078})} \;\catD, \nonumber \\[4pt]
  a_{1/2}^{(N)} &= b_0 - \frac{1}{2} = +\frac{1}{2}, \qquad
  a_{1/2}^{(D)} = b_1 - \frac{1}{2} = -\frac{1}{2}, \qquad
  \left|\partial\mathcal{S}_{t_2}\right| = 2\left(a_{1/2}^{(N)} - a_{1/2}^{(D)}\right) = 2 \;\;\catD, \nonumber \\[4pt]
  \lim_{s \to 0^+}\sqrt{s}&\left[\Theta_{\Omega^0}^{(N)}(s; \Phi) - \Theta_{\mathrm{SS}}^{(-)}(s; \Phi)\right] = 0
  \qquad \textnormal{(\texttt{COR~\#079})} \;\;\catD. \label{eq:c4_mckean_singer_supertrace}
  \end{align}
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Writing $\sum_{n=0}^\infty e^{-s n^2/(L_0\Phi)^2} = \frac{1}{2}\sum_{n=-\infty}^\infty e^{-s n^2/(L_0\Phi)^2} + \frac{1}{2}$ and applying the Poisson summation formula $\sum_{n=-\infty}^\infty e^{-s n^2/(L_0\Phi)^2} = \frac{\sqrt{\pi}L_0\Phi}{\sqrt{s}}\sum_{w=-\infty}^\infty e^{-\pi^2 w^2 L_0^2\Phi^2/s}$ separates $w = 0$ from $w \ge 1$ to establish Eqs.~\eqref{eq:c4_poisson_winding_sum}--\eqref{eq:c4_winding_remainder_def}. Subtracting $\Theta_{\Omega^1}^{(D)}(s) = b_1 + \sum_{n=1}^\infty e^{-s\lambda_n}$ from $\Theta_{\Omega^0}^{(N)}(s) = b_0 + \sum_{n=1}^\infty e^{-s\lambda_n}$ cancels all $n \ge 1$ modes identically, proving Eq.~\eqref{eq:c4_mckean_singer_supertrace}.
\end{proof}

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{The Spectral Zeta Function $\zeta_{\mathcal{S}_{t_2}}(z; \Phi)$, Euler--Maclaurin and Inductive Partial-Sum Proof of the Ray--Singer Determinant, $\widehat{\mathbf{Q}}_{17}$, and $\zeta_R(5)$ Winding Integral (\texttt{THM~\#17, COR~\#073})}{The Spectral Zeta Function, Euler-Maclaurin Proof of the Ray-Singer Determinant, Q\_17, and zeta\_R(5) Winding Integral}}

\begin{theorem}[Euler--Maclaurin and Inductive Partial-Sum Proof of Ray--Singer Torsion $\ln(2\pi L_0\Phi)$, $\zeta_R(5)$ Winding Density, and Bulk-Plus-Boundary Grading Operator $\widehat{\mathbf{Q}}_{17}(\Phi)$ --- \texttt{THM~\#17, COR~\#073} \catD\ / \catH]\label{thm:c4_zeta_winding_q17_identities}
\leavevmode
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
  \item \textbf{Fiber Spectral Zeta Function and Euler--Maclaurin / Inductive Proof of the Ray--Singer Determinant (\texttt{COR~\#073}):}
  For $\mathrm{Re}(z) > \frac{1}{2}$, $\zeta_{\mathcal{S}_{t_2}}(z; \Phi) \equiv \sum_{n=1}^{\infty} \lambda_n(\Phi)^{-z} = (L_0\Phi)^{2z}\zeta_R(2z)$. For $\mathrm{Re}(s) > 1$, integrating by parts on each unit interval $[n, n+1]$ ($n \ge 1$) with the verified antiderivative $\frac{d}{dx}\big[(x - n - \frac{1}{2})\frac{x^{-s}}{-s} - \frac{x^{1-s}}{(-s)(1-s)}\big] = (x - n - \frac{1}{2})x^{-s-1}$ and summing over $n \ge 1$ deduces from the Dirichlet series the exact Euler--Maclaurin representation:
  \begin{equation}\label{eq:c4_euler_maclaurin_zeta}
  \zeta_R(s) = \frac{1}{s - 1} + \frac{1}{2} - s\,J(s), \qquad
  J(s) \equiv \int_1^\infty \left(\{x\} - \frac{1}{2}\right)x^{-s-1}\,dx \;\;\catD.
  \end{equation}
  Because $J(s)$ converges for $\mathrm{Re}(s) > -1$, by the uniqueness of analytic continuation Eq.~\eqref{eq:c4_euler_maclaurin_zeta} extends to $\mathrm{Re}(s) > -1$. Evaluating at $s = 0$ immediately gives $\zeta_R(0) = -1 + \frac{1}{2} = -\frac{1}{2}$, and differentiating at $s = 0$ gives $\zeta'_R(0) = -1 - J(0)$. On $[n, n+1]$, $\int_n^{n+1}\frac{x - n - 1/2}{x}\,dx = \big[x - (n+\frac{1}{2})\ln x\big]_n^{n+1}$, whose partial sums $I_N \equiv \sum_{n=1}^{N-1}\int_n^{n+1}\frac{x-n-1/2}{x}\,dx = (N - 1) - (N + \frac{1}{2})\ln N + \ln(N!)$ are proved by exact mathematical induction ($I_1 = 0$ and $I_{N+1} - I_N = 1 - (N + \frac{1}{2})\ln(\frac{N+1}{N})$) and converge as $N \to \infty$ to $J(0) = \frac{1}{2}\ln(2\pi) - 1$, proving $\zeta'_R(0) = -1 - (\frac{1}{2}\ln(2\pi) - 1) = -\frac{1}{2}\ln(2\pi)$ and yielding at $z = 0$:
  \begin{align}
  \ln\det'\!\left(-\frac{1}{\Phi^2}\partial_2^2\right) &\equiv -\left.\frac{d}{dz}\zeta_{\mathcal{S}_{t_2}}(z; \Phi)\right|_{z=0} = -\left[2\ln(L_0\Phi)\,\zeta_R(0) + 2\,\zeta'_R(0)\right] \nonumber \\[3pt]
  &= \ln\!\left(2\pi L_0\,\Phi\right) = \ln\!\left(2\pi L_0\,\Phi_\infty\right) + \psi \;\;\catD. \label{eq:c4_raysinger_linear_psi}
  \end{align}
  \item \textbf{Closed-Form 5D Winding Density ($\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{wind}}(\Phi)$) and Rescaled Density ($\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{wind}}(\psi)$):}
  \begin{align}
  \mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{wind}}(\Phi) &\equiv -\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{\infty} \frac{ds}{s}\,\frac{1}{(4\pi s)^2}\,\widetilde{\Theta}_{\mathrm{wind}}(s; \Phi) = -\frac{3\,\zeta_R(5)}{128\,\pi^6\,L_0^4\,\Phi^4} \;\;\catD, \label{eq:c4_vwind_jordan} \\[4pt]
  \widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{wind}}(\psi) &\equiv e^{-2\psi}\,\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{wind}}\!\left(\Phi_\infty e^\psi\right) = -\frac{3\,\zeta_R(5)}{128\,\pi^6\,L_0^4\,\Phi_\infty^4}\,e^{-6\psi} \;\;\catD. \label{eq:c4_vwind_einstein}
  \end{align}
  \item \textbf{The $17\times 17$ Bulk-Plus-Boundary McKean--Singer Grading Operator $\widehat{\mathbf{Q}}_{17}(\Phi) \equiv \Phi^{-1}\mathbb{I}_{15} \oplus (2\Phi)^{-1}\mathbb{I}_2$ (\catD\ / \catH):}
  While inside the homogeneous tensor square $V_5^* \otimes V_5^* = \mathrm{Sym}^2(15) \oplus \Lambda^2(10) = 25$ the $\mathbb{Z}_2$-even subspace has canonical dimension $11_+ (\mathrm{Sym}^2_+) + 6_+ (\Lambda^2_+) = 17_+$ vs.\ $8_-$ (\catD), pairing the $N_{\mathrm{sym}} = 15$ bulk metric configuration modes (Weyl weight $\Phi^{-1}$) with the $N_\partial = |\partial\mathcal{S}_{t_2}| = 2$ Neumann mirror endpoints $\{0, \pi L_0\}$ (weighted by the McKean--Singer defect $a_{1/2}^{(N)} = \frac{1}{2}$ of Eq.~\eqref{eq:c4_mckean_singer_supertrace}, weight $(2\Phi)^{-1}$) defines the composite configuration-boundary grading operator $\widehat{\mathbf{Q}}_{17}(\Phi) \equiv \Phi^{-1}\mathbb{I}_{15} \oplus (2\Phi)^{-1}\mathbb{I}_2$ (\catH), whose matrix invariants are (\catD):
  \begin{align}
  \mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{config}} &= 15 + 2 = 17, \qquad
  \frac{\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{config}}}{N_{\mathrm{sym}}} = \frac{17}{15} \;\;\catH, \nonumber \\[3pt]
  \mathrm{Tr}\!\left(\widehat{\mathbf{Q}}_{17}(\Phi)\right) &= \frac{16}{\Phi} \;\;\catD, \qquad
  \det\!\left(\widehat{\mathbf{Q}}_{17}(\Phi)\right) = \frac{1}{4}\,\Phi^{-17} \;\;\catD. \label{eq:c4_q17_invariants}
  \end{align}
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\subsection{Twisted Jacobi $\vartheta_2 \leftrightarrow \vartheta_4$ Modular Duality for $\mathcal{M}_2^{(-)}(\Phi) = \frac{\ln 2}{16\pi^2}$, Regulator-Independent Jet Basis of $\mathcal{W}_3$, Cauchy--Bernstein Gaussian Kernel $\psi_t(\mathcal{A})$, and Radon--Plancherel $\mathcal{C}^3$ Sphere-Overlap Polynomial $f(\lambda)$ (\texttt{THM~\#17 \& \#18, COR~\#074--\#077})}

\begin{theorem}[{Twisted Scherk--Schwarz Jacobi $\vartheta_2 \leftrightarrow \vartheta_4$ Modular Proof of $\mathcal{M}_2^{(-)}(\Phi) = \frac{\ln 2}{16\pi^2}$ Under Axiom~4.1, Regulator Independence, Non-Singular Jet Isomorphism ($\det\mathbf{M}_{\mathrm{jet}}^{\mathrm{raw}} = -16$, $\det\mathbf{M}_{\mathrm{jet}} = -\frac{32}{3}$), Unique Shifted Basis Decomposition of $\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{spec}}(\psi, \mathcal{A})$, Cauchy--Bernstein Gaussian Kernel $\psi_t(\mathcal{A})$, and Radon--Plancherel $\mathcal{C}^3$ Sphere-Overlap Polynomial $f(\lambda)$ ($\Delta f_{\mathrm{mut}}^{(4)}(1) = -30$) --- \texttt{THM~\#17 \& \#18, COR~\#074--\#077} \catD}]\label{thm:c4_moments_and_potential}
Under the dimensionless proper-time substitution $\hat{s} \equiv s/(L_0\Phi)^2$, the $k$-th Seeley--DeWitt Mellin integral $\mathcal{M}_k^{(w)}(\Phi) \equiv \int_0^\infty \frac{ds}{s}(4\pi s)^{-2}s^k\,\widetilde{\Theta}_{\mathrm{wind}}^{(w)}(s;\Phi)$ scales strictly as $(L_0\Phi)^{-(4-2k)}$ for every $k \in \{0, 1, 2\}$, evaluating for the $w$-th dihedral reflection image ($w \in \mathbb{Z}_+$) to $\frac{\Gamma(\frac{5}{2}-k)}{16\pi^{13/2-2k}(L_0\Phi)^{4-2k}}w^{-(5-2k)}$ with winding exponent $p_k = 5 - 2k \in \{5, 3, 1\}$ \catD. Under the orbifold parity decomposition $\mathrm{Sym}^2(T^*\mathcal{M}_{3,2}) = \mathrm{Sym}^2_+ \oplus \mathrm{Sym}^2_-$ of Theorem~\ref{thm:c1_rz2_projection}(ii) and Axiom~\ref{ax:axiom_two} (\texttt{AXIOM\_2}), the two subbundles carry complementary modular heat traces on $\mathcal{S}_{t_2} = S^1_{t_2}/\mathbb{Z}_2 = [0, \pi L_0]$:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
  \item \textbf{Untwisted $\mathbb{Z}_2$-Even Subbundle $\mathrm{Sym}^2_+$ ($11$ modes, $\epsilon_A\epsilon_B = +1$):} Periodic integer harmonics $k_{2,n}^{(+)} = n/L_0$ ($n \ge 0$) yield the $\vartheta_3$ trace~\eqref{eq:c4_poisson_winding_sum} with positive winding weights $+1$, converging absolutely for $k \in \{0, 1\}$ to $\sum_{w=1}^\infty w^{-(5-2k)} = \zeta_R(5 - 2k)$, whereas at $k = 2$ ($p_2 = 1$) the untwisted sum diverges harmonically as $\sum_{w=1}^\infty w^{-1} = \zeta_R(1) = \infty$ and is discarded by Axiom~\ref{ax:axiom_two} \catD;
  \item \textbf{Twisted $\mathbb{Z}_2$-Odd Subbundle $\mathrm{Sym}^2_-$ ($4$ off-diagonal modes $g_{\mu 2}$, $\epsilon_\mu\epsilon_2 = -1$, \texttt{COR~\#074}):} Under the antiperiodic Scherk--Schwarz reflection character $\chi(\sigma_\pi\sigma_0) = -1$ of $\pi_1^{\mathrm{orb}}(\mathcal{S}_{t_2}) = \mathbb{Z}_2 * \mathbb{Z}_2 \simeq D_\infty$ (which removes the $n = 0$ zero mode and shifts the odd fiber spectrum to half-integer wavenumbers $k_{2,n}^{(-)} = (n + \frac{1}{2})/L_0$, $n \ge 0$), Jacobi's $\vartheta_2 \leftrightarrow \vartheta_4$ modular Poisson transformation ($\int_{-\infty}^\infty e^{-s(u+1/2)^2/(L_0\Phi)^2}e^{-2\pi i w u}du = e^{i\pi w}\frac{\sqrt{\pi}L_0\Phi}{\sqrt{s}}e^{-\pi^2 w^2 L_0^2\Phi^2/s}$ with torsion phase $e^{i\pi w} = (-1)^w$) yields the heat trace $\Theta_{\mathcal{S}_{t_2}}^{(-)}(s;\Phi) = \frac{1}{2}\vartheta_2(0\mid\frac{is}{\pi L_0^2\Phi^2}) = \frac{\sqrt{\pi}L_0\Phi}{2\sqrt{s}}\vartheta_4(0\mid\frac{i\pi L_0^2\Phi^2}{s}) = \frac{\pi L_0\Phi}{\sqrt{4\pi s}} + \widetilde{\Theta}_{\mathrm{wind}}^{(-)}(s;\Phi)$, where defining the twisted moment $\mathcal{M}_k^{(-)}(\Phi)$ with the explicit minus sign $[-\widetilde{\Theta}_{\mathrm{wind}}^{(-)}(s;\Phi)] = \frac{\sqrt{\pi}L_0\Phi}{\sqrt{s}}\sum_{w=1}^\infty (-1)^{w-1}e^{-\pi^2 w^2 L_0^2\Phi^2/s}$ (so that the $w = 1$ leading reflection term has weight $+1$) produces exact alternating weights $-(-1)^w = (-1)^{w-1}$ and zero boundary defect, converging at $k = 2$ to Dirichlet's eta value $\eta(1) = \sum_{w=1}^\infty (-1)^{w-1}/w = \ln 2 < \infty$ (supplying the finite non-vanishing $\Phi^0$ moment under Axiom~\ref{ax:axiom_two}) \catD:
\end{enumerate}
\begin{align}
\mathcal{M}_k(\Phi) &= \frac{\Gamma(\frac{5}{2} - k)\,\zeta_R(5 - 2k)}{16\,\pi^{\frac{13}{2}-2k}(L_0\Phi)^{4-2k}} \propto \Phi^{-(4-2k)} \qquad (k = 0, 1) \;\;\catD, \label{eq:c4_mellin_moments_01} \\[4pt]
\mathcal{M}_0(\Phi) &= \frac{3\,\zeta_R(5)}{64\pi^6(L_0\Phi)^4}, \qquad
\mathcal{M}_1(\Phi) = \frac{\zeta_R(3)}{32\pi^4(L_0\Phi)^2} \;\;\catD, \nonumber \\[4pt]
\mathcal{M}_2^{(-)}(\Phi) &\equiv \int_0^\infty\frac{ds}{s}\frac{s^2}{(4\pi s)^2}\left[-\widetilde{\Theta}_{\mathrm{wind}}^{(-)}(s;\Phi)\right] = \frac{\eta(1)}{16\pi^2}\,\Phi^0 = \frac{\ln 2}{16\pi^2}\,\Phi^0 \;\;\catD. \label{eq:c4_mellin_moments_values}
\end{align}
Multiplying the three resulting fiber powers $\{\Phi^0, \Phi^{-2}, \Phi^{-4}\}\big|_{\Phi=\Phi_\infty e^\psi} = \{1, e^{-2\psi}, e^{-4\psi}\}$ by the 4D Weyl Jacobian $e^{-2\psi}$ of Eq.~\eqref{eq:c3_volume_rescaling} spans the three-dimensional exponential potential space $\mathcal{W}_3 \equiv \mathrm{Span}\{e^{-2\psi}, e^{-4\psi}, e^{-6\psi}\}$ stipulated in Axiom~\ref{ax:axiom_two} (\catA) ($\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{spec}}(\psi) = c_0 e^{-2\psi} + c_1 e^{-4\psi} + c_2 e^{-6\psi}$), where the explicit evaluation $\mathcal{M}_2^{(-)}(\Phi) = \frac{\ln 2}{16\pi^2}\Phi^0 \neq 0$ in Eq.~\eqref{eq:c4_mellin_moments_values} proves that the $k = 2$ ($\Phi^0 \mapsto e^{-2\psi}$) Mellin moment is finite and strictly non-zero (whereas its specific numerical value $\frac{\ln 2}{16\pi^2}$ and sign, together with the non-zero prefactors $\mu_k$, drop out identically once normalized to the vacuum 2-jet below). At $\psi = 0$, the raw Wronskian 2-jet matrix $\mathbf{M}_{\mathrm{jet}}^{\mathrm{raw}}$ on $(f(0), f'(0), f''(0))^T$ is a Vandermonde matrix on $(-2, -4, -6)$ with determinant $\det(\mathbf{M}_{\mathrm{jet}}^{\mathrm{raw}}) = -16 \neq 0$ (\texttt{COR~\#075}), and the scaled evaluation map $j^2_0[\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{spec}}] \equiv (\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{spec}}(0), \mathcal{V}'_{\mathrm{spec}}(0), \frac{2}{3}\mathcal{V}''_{\mathrm{spec}}(0))^T = \mathbf{M}_{\mathrm{jet}}(c_0, c_1, c_2)^T$ has determinant $\det(\mathbf{M}_{\mathrm{jet}}) = \frac{2}{3}(-16) = -\frac{32}{3} \neq 0$ \catD. Consequently:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
  \item \textbf{Regulator-Shape Independence and Unique Biorthogonal Jet Basis Determination of $\mathcal{W}_3$ (\texttt{COR~\#075--\#076}):}
  For any non-zero spectral cutoff moment prefactors $(\mu_0, \mu_1, \mu_2) \in (\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\})^3$ in $\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{reg}}(\psi) = b_0\mu_2 e^{-2\psi} + b_1\mu_1 e^{-4\psi} + b_2\mu_0 e^{-6\psi}$ (including any rescaling of $\mu_0, \mu_1$ if the finite twisted moments $\eta(5)=\frac{15}{16}\zeta_R(5)$ and $\eta(3)=\frac{3}{4}\zeta_R(3)$ of $\mathrm{Sym}^2_-$ were added at $k \in \{0,1\}$), invertibility of $\mathbf{M}_{\mathrm{jet}}$ ($\det\mathbf{M}_{\mathrm{jet}}^{\mathrm{raw}} = -16 \neq 0$) establishes a linear isomorphism between the coefficient space $\mathbb{R}^3$ and the vacuum 2-jet space $J^2_0(\mathcal{W}_3)$: once the asymptotic vacuum scales $(\mathcal{V}_0, m_\psi^2)$ are specified under the vacuum equilibrium condition $\mathcal{V}'_{\mathrm{spec}}(0) = 0$, the 2-jet $(3\mathcal{V}_0, 0, m_\psi^2)^T$ uniquely fixes $b_k \propto \mu_{2-k}^{-1}$ with zero remaining free structural parameters, so that $\frac{\partial\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{reg}}}{\partial\mu_k} \equiv 0$ identically ($k \in \{0, 1, 2\}$): the basis polynomials $\Pi_0(\psi)$ and $\Pi_2(\psi)$ spanning $\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{spec}} \in \mathcal{W}_3$ are completely independent of the shape of the ultraviolet spectral regulator $f(u)$ \catD. Explicitly, $\mathbf{M}_{\mathrm{jet}}(c_0, c_1, c_2)^T = (3\mathcal{V}_0, 0, m_\psi^2)^T$ has the unique algebraic solution:
  \begin{equation}\label{eq:c4_jet_coefficients}
  c_0 = 9\mathcal{V}_0 + \frac{3}{16}m_\psi^2, \qquad
  c_1 = -9\mathcal{V}_0 - \frac{3}{8}m_\psi^2, \qquad
  c_2 = 3\mathcal{V}_0 + \frac{3}{16}m_\psi^2 \;\;\catD,
  \end{equation}
  decomposing $\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{spec}}(\psi) = 3\mathcal{V}_0\Pi_0(\psi) + \frac{3}{2}m_\psi^2\Pi_2(\psi)$ into the canonical biorthogonal basis polynomials $\Pi_0(\psi) = e^{-2\psi}(3 - 3e^{-2\psi} + e^{-4\psi})$ and $\Pi_2(\psi) = \frac{1}{8}e^{-2\psi}(1 - e^{-2\psi})^2$ ($\langle J^a, \Pi_b\rangle = \delta^a_b$, \texttt{COR~\#075}):
  \begin{equation}\label{eq:c4_unshifted_potential}
  \mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{spec}}(\psi) = 3\mathcal{V}_0\,e^{-2\psi}\left(3 - 3e^{-2\psi} + e^{-4\psi}\right) + \frac{3}{16}m_\psi^2\,e^{-2\psi}\left(1 - e^{-2\psi}\right)^2 \;\;\catD.
  \end{equation}
  For any khronon-dependent equilibrium root $\psi = \psi_t(\mathcal{A})$ ($\partial_\psi\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{spec}}\big|_{\psi=\psi_t(\mathcal{A})} = 0$, stipulated by Axiom~\ref{ax:axiom_three}), translating the basis polynomials to the shifted argument $\Delta\psi \equiv \psi - \psi_t(\mathcal{A})$ preserves the exact second-order jet $(3\mathcal{V}_0, 0, \frac{3}{2}m_\psi^2)$ around $\psi = \psi_t(\mathcal{A})$ for all $\mathcal{V}_0$ and $\psi_t(\mathcal{A})$ (\texttt{COR~\#076}):
  \begin{align}
  \mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{spec}}(\psi, \mathcal{A}) &= 3\mathcal{V}_0\,\Pi_0\!\left(\psi - \psi_t(\mathcal{A})\right) + \frac{3}{2}m_\psi^2\,\Pi_2\!\left(\psi - \psi_t(\mathcal{A})\right) \nonumber \\[3pt]
  &= 3\mathcal{V}_0\,e^{-2\Delta\psi}\left(3 - 3e^{-2\Delta\psi} + e^{-4\Delta\psi}\right) + \frac{3}{16}m_\psi^2\,e^{-2\Delta\psi}\left(1 - e^{-2\Delta\psi}\right)^2 \nonumber \\[3pt]
  &= 3\mathcal{V}_0 + \frac{3}{4}m_\psi^2\left[\psi - \psi_t(\mathcal{A})\right]^2 + \mathcal{O}(\Delta\psi^3) \quad \textnormal{(\texttt{COR~\#076})} \;\catD, \label{eq:c4_shifted_potential}
  \end{align}
  so that Eq.~\eqref{eq:c3_master_scalar_eq} linearizes around $\psi_t(\mathcal{A})$ to $\tilde{\Delta}_4\psi - m_\psi^2[\psi - \psi_t(\mathcal{A})] = +\frac{1}{3}\tilde{T}_{(\mathrm{traj})}$ \catD.
  \item \textbf{Cauchy--Bernstein Semigroup Characterization of the Even Gaussian Kernel $\psi_t(\mathcal{A})$ (\texttt{COR~\#076} \catD\ \& Constitutive Semigroup/Threshold Identification \catH):}
  Because $\mathcal{A}(r)$ changes sign smoothly across a simple zero at $r = r_h$ ($\mathcal{A}(r_h) = 0$), under the constitutive symmetry condition (\catH) that the equilibrium shift is $\mathbb{Z}_2$-even in $\mathcal{A}$ (Whitney's theorem: $\psi_t(\mathcal{A}) = \tilde{\psi}_t(u)$ with $u \equiv \mathcal{A}^2 \ge 0$, guaranteeing $\left.\frac{d\psi_t}{d\mathcal{A}}\right|_{\mathcal{A}=0} = 0$) and that the normalized attenuation factor $F(u)$ obeys Cauchy's memoryless additive semigroup law $F(u + v) = F(u)F(v)$ with $F(0) = 1$ and $F'(0) = -\lambda_A < 0$, Cauchy's functional theorem deductively forces the unique ODE $F'(u) = -\lambda_A F(u)$ with completely monotone Bernstein solution $F(u) = e^{-\lambda_A u}$ ($(-1)^k\frac{d^k F}{du^k} = \lambda_A^k e^{-\lambda_A u} > 0$, \texttt{COR~\#076}) \catD. Equivalently, with general amplitude $\psi_0$ and attenuation rate $\lambda_A \equiv A_{\mathrm{crit}}^{-2}$:
  \begin{align}
  \frac{d}{d\mathcal{A}}\left[\psi_t(\mathcal{A}) - \psi_\infty\right] &= -2\lambda_A\mathcal{A}\left[\psi_t(\mathcal{A}) - \psi_\infty\right] \;\;\catD \nonumber \\[3pt]
  \Longrightarrow\quad \psi_t(\mathcal{A}) - \psi_\infty &= \psi_0\exp\!\left(-\lambda_A\mathcal{A}^2\right) \quad \textnormal{(\texttt{COR~\#076})} \;\catD, \nonumber \\[4pt]
  \psi_t(\mathcal{A}) &= \psi_{\max}\left[\exp\!\left(-\frac{\mathcal{A}^2}{A_{\mathrm{crit}}^2}\right) - \exp\!\left(-\frac{1}{A_{\mathrm{crit}}^2}\right)\right] \quad \left(\psi_0 = \frac{2}{25},\; A_{\mathrm{crit}} = \frac{1}{4}\;\catH\right), \label{eq:c4_gaussian_kernel}
  \end{align}
  where the implication $F(u+v) = F(u)F(v) \implies \psi_0 e^{-\lambda_A\mathcal{A}^2}$ is a pure mathematical theorem (\catD), while imposing memoryless semigroup composition in $u = \mathcal{A}^2$ and fixing $(\psi_0, \lambda_A^{-1/2})$ to the cross-order endomorphism ratios $(\psi_{\max} = 2/25, A_{\mathrm{crit}} = 1/4)$ of Eq.~\eqref{eq:c1_trace_ratios} are constitutive bridge identifications (\catH).
  \item \textbf{Radon--Plancherel Potential Convolution Polynomial $f(\lambda)$ of Two Homogeneous Balls in $\mathbb{R}^3$, $\mathcal{C}^3$ Contact Smoothness, and 4th-Derivative Jump $-30$ (\texttt{COR~\#077}):}
  For a uniform ball $B_R(\mathbf{0})$ in $\mathbb{R}^3$ of radius $R > 0$ and unit mass ($\rho_0 = \frac{3}{4\pi R^3}$), the 1D Radon slice projection $\mathcal{R}[\rho_B](z) = \frac{3}{4R}(1 - z^2/R^2)\mathbf{1}_{|z|\le R}$ has Fourier transform $\hat{\rho}_B(k) = \frac{3(\sin kR - kR\cos kR)}{(kR)^3}$ and mutual gravitational interaction energy $W(d) = \frac{2}{d}\int_0^d C_{\mathrm{rad}}(u)\,du$ at center separation $d \equiv 2R\lambda \ge 0$, with self-energy $W(0) = \frac{6}{5R}$ and exact Newton shell theorem $d\cdot W(d) = 1$ for $d \ge 2R$ ($\lambda \ge 1$). Consequently, the normalized mutual interaction profile $f_{\mathrm{mut}}(\lambda) \equiv W(2R\lambda)/W(0)$ and the complementary self-energy difference $f(\lambda) \equiv \frac{6}{5}[1 - f_{\mathrm{mut}}(\lambda)]$ satisfy exact $\mathcal{C}^3$ contact continuity across $\lambda = 1$ (where $f_{\mathrm{mut}}^{(k)}(1^-) = f_{\mathrm{mut}}^{(k)}(1^+)$ for all $k \in \{0, 1, 2, 3\}$ with $f_{\mathrm{mut}}^{(3)}(1) = -\frac{5}{2}$) and exhibit their first non-vanishing discontinuity at the fourth derivative ($\Delta f_{\mathrm{mut}}^{(4)}(1) = -20 - 10 = -30$, \texttt{COR~\#077}):
  \begin{align}
  f_{\mathrm{mut}}(\lambda) \equiv \frac{W(2R\lambda)}{W(0)} &= \begin{cases}
  1 - \frac{5}{3}\lambda^2 + \frac{5}{4}\lambda^3 - \frac{1}{6}\lambda^5 & (0 \le \lambda \le 1), \\[3pt]
  \frac{5}{12\lambda} & (\lambda \ge 1) \;\;\catD,
  \end{cases} \nonumber \\[4pt]
  \Delta f_{\mathrm{mut}}^{(k)}(1) &= 0 \quad (\forall\,k \in \{0, 1, 2, 3\}), \qquad
  \Delta f_{\mathrm{mut}}^{(4)}(1) = -30 \quad \textnormal{(\texttt{COR~\#077})} \;\catD, \nonumber \\[4pt]
  f(\lambda) = \frac{6}{5}\left[1 - f_{\mathrm{mut}}(\lambda)\right]
  &= \begin{cases}
  2\lambda^2 - \frac{3}{2}\lambda^3 + \frac{1}{5}\lambda^5 & (0 \le \lambda \le 1), \\[3pt]
  \frac{6}{5} - \frac{1}{2\lambda} & (\lambda \ge 1) \;\;\catD,
  \end{cases} \label{eq:c4_ball_overlap_quintic}
  \end{align}
  which is $\mathcal{C}^3$-smooth across tangential contact $\lambda = 1$ with $f(1) = \frac{7}{10}$, $f'(1) = \frac{1}{2}$, $f''(1) = -1$, $f'''(1) = +3$, and $\Delta f^{(4)}(1) = +36$ \catD.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{The Explicit Seeley--DeWitt Curvature Polynomials ($a_0, a_1, a_2$) and the 5D Gauss--Bonnet Invariant $\mathcal{G}_5$ (\texttt{THM~\#16, COR~\#070--\#071})}{The Explicit Seeley-DeWitt Curvature Polynomials and the 5D Gauss-Bonnet Invariant G\_5}}

\begin{theorem}[Exact Non-Diagonal Reduction of $\mathcal{R}_2^{(5)}$, the Gauss--Bonnet Hessian Cancellation $\mathcal{G}_5 = \mathcal{G}_4 + \frac{8}{\Phi}G^{(4)\mu\nu}\nabla_\mu\partial_\nu\Phi$ ($c_{\mathrm{GB}}=8$), and Explicit Seeley--DeWitt Coefficients --- \texttt{THM~\#16, COR~\#070--\#071} \catD]\label{thm:c4_gb_seeley_dewitt_identities}\label{thm:c4_gauss_bonnet_cancellation}
On $\mathcal{M}_{3,2}$ with $\partial_2\mathbf{g}_{\mathrm{proj}} = 0$, the quadratic Ricci scalar $\mathcal{R}_2^{(5)} \equiv R_{AB}^{(5)}R^{(5)AB}$, the five-dimensional Lovelock--Gauss--Bonnet scalar $\mathcal{G}_5 \equiv \mathcal{K}_5 - 4\mathcal{R}_2^{(5)} + (R^{(5)})^2$, and the scalar Gilkey--DeWitt coefficients $a_0 = 1$, $a_1 = \frac{1}{6}R^{(5)}$, and $a_2 = \frac{1}{180}\big[\mathcal{K}_5 - \mathcal{R}_2^{(5)} + \frac{5}{2}(R^{(5)})^2 - 6\Delta_5 R^{(5)}\big]$ satisfy:
\begin{align}
\mathcal{R}_2^{(5)} &= R_{\mu\nu}^{(4)}R^{(4)\mu\nu} - \frac{2}{\Phi}\,R^{(4)\mu\nu}\!\left({}^{(4)}\nabla_\mu\partial_\nu\Phi\right) \nonumber \\[3pt]
&\quad + \frac{1}{\Phi^2}\left[\left({}^{(4)}\nabla_\mu\partial_\nu\Phi\right)^2 + \left(\Delta_4\Phi\right)^2\right] \qquad \textnormal{(\texttt{COR~\#070})} \;\;\catD, \label{eq:c4_r2_split} \\[4pt]
\mathcal{G}_5 &= \mathcal{G}_4 + \frac{c_{\mathrm{GB}}}{\Phi}\,G^{(4)\mu\nu}\!\left({}^{(4)}\nabla_\mu\partial_\nu\Phi\right) \qquad \left(c_{\mathrm{GB}} = 8, \;\; \textnormal{\texttt{COR~\#071}}\right) \;\;\catD, \label{eq:c4_gb_split} \\[4pt]
\sqrt{|g_5|}\,\mathcal{G}_5 &= \Phi\sqrt{-g_4}\,\mathcal{G}_4 + 8\,\partial_\mu\!\left(\sqrt{-g_4}\,G^{(4)\mu\nu}\partial_\nu\Phi\right) \qquad \textnormal{(\texttt{COR~\#071})} \;\;\catD, \label{eq:c4_gb_volume_divergence} \\[4pt]
a_2 &= a_2^{(4)} + \frac{1}{60\,\Phi^2}\left[\left({}^{(4)}\nabla_\mu\partial_\nu\Phi\right)^2 + 3\left(\Delta_4\Phi\right)^2\right] \nonumber \\[3pt]
&\quad + \frac{1}{90\,\Phi}\left(R^{(4)\mu\nu} - 5g^{\mu\nu}R^{(4)}\right){}^{(4)}\nabla_\mu\partial_\nu\Phi - \frac{1}{30}\left(\Delta_5 R^{(5)} - \Delta_4 R^{(4)}\right) \;\;\catD, \label{eq:c4_a1_a2_explicit}
\end{align}
cancelling all quadratic second derivatives $({}^{(4)}\nabla_\mu\partial_\nu\Phi)^2$ and $(\Delta_4\Phi)^2$ in $\mathcal{G}_5$ identically (zero fourth-order Ostrogradsky ghosts).
\end{theorem}

\begin{remark}[Identification of Section~4 Curvature and Spectral Invariants with Recognized Mathematical Theorems]\label{rem:c4_master_recognition}
The five curvature and spectral structures deduced in Section~4 correspond to classical theorems of Riemannian and spectral geometry:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\arabic*.]
  \item \textbf{Gauss--Codazzi--Mainardi Kretschmann Split and Gibbons--Hawking--York (GHY) Holonomy (Theorem~\ref{thm:c4_kretschmann_ghy_identities}):} Equation~\eqref{eq:c4_kretschmann_split} proves that 5D pointwise curvature regularity ($|\mathcal{K}_5| < \infty$) requires both $|\mathcal{K}_4| < \infty$ and bounded Hessian norm $\frac{4}{\Phi^2}({}^{(4)}\nabla_\mu\partial_\nu\Phi)^2 < \infty$. Equation~\eqref{eq:c4_extrinsic_trace_split} ($K = K^{(4)} + n^\mu\partial_\mu\psi$ for the unit normal $n^A = g^{Ar}/\sqrt{g^{rr}}$) cancels the bulk divergence $-2\oint n^\mu\partial_\mu\Phi$ and generates $\Delta\ell_\partial^{(n)} = 2n\ln\Phi_\infty$ ($\exp(\Delta\ell_\partial^{(n)}) = \Phi_\infty^{2n}$) across $n$ reflections in $\pi_1^{\mathrm{orb}}(\mathcal{S}_{t_2}) \simeq D_\infty$ \catD.
  \item \textbf{Jacobi $\vartheta_3$ and $\vartheta_2 \leftrightarrow \vartheta_4$ Modular Inversions and the McKean--Singer De Rham Supertrace $\mathrm{Str} = +1$ (Theorems~\ref{thm:c4_jacobi_poisson_weyl} and~\ref{thm:c4_moments_and_potential}):} Equations~\eqref{eq:c4_poisson_winding_sum}--\eqref{eq:c4_mckean_singer_supertrace} and~\eqref{eq:c4_mellin_moments_values} apply Jacobi's modular transformations and the McKean--Singer De Rham cohomological supertrace \cite{McKeanSinger1967} ($\Theta_{\Omega^0}^{(N)} - \Theta_{\Omega^1}^{(D)} = b_0 - b_1 = +1$) to $\mathcal{S}_{t_2}$, deductively proving $a_{1/2}^{(N)} = +\frac{1}{2}$, $a_{1/2}^{(D)} = -\frac{1}{2}$, $|\partial\mathcal{S}_{t_2}| = 2$, and exact alternating weights $(-1)^{w-1}$ converging to $\eta(1) = \ln 2$ at $k = 2$ (under the sign convention of $[-\widetilde{\Theta}_{\mathrm{wind}}^{(-)}]$ in Eq.~\eqref{eq:c4_mellin_moments_values}) so as to maintain ultraviolet-finite Mellin winding moments under \textbf{Axiom~\ref{ax:axiom_two}} \catD.
  \item \textbf{Euler--Maclaurin and Inductive Partial-Sum Proof of Ray--Singer Torsion and Appelquist--Chodos Epstein $\zeta_R(5)$ Integral (Theorem~\ref{thm:c4_zeta_winding_q17_identities}):} Equations~\eqref{eq:c4_euler_maclaurin_zeta}--\eqref{eq:c4_raysinger_linear_psi} deduce $\zeta_R(s) = \frac{1}{s-1} + \frac{1}{2} - s J(s)$ from the Dirichlet series and prove the closed partial sums of $J(0) = \frac{1}{2}\ln(2\pi) - 1$ by induction (under analytic continuation to $\mathrm{Re}(s) > -1$) to establish $\zeta_R(0)=-1/2$, $\zeta'_R(0)=-\frac{1}{2}\ln(2\pi)$, and the \emph{Ray--Singer functional determinant} \cite{RaySinger1971}, while Eq.~\eqref{eq:c4_vwind_jordan} is the \emph{Epstein--Casimir $\zeta_R(5)$ density} \cite{AppelquistChodos1983} \catD.
  \item \textbf{Regulator-Independent Jet-Space Isomorphism on $\mathcal{W}_3$, Cauchy--Bernstein Gaussian Law, and $\mathcal{C}^3$ Sphere Overlap Polynomial $f(\lambda)$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:c4_moments_and_potential}):} Because $\det(\mathbf{M}_{\mathrm{jet}}^{\mathrm{raw}}) = -16 \neq 0$ ($\det\mathbf{M}_{\mathrm{jet}} = -\frac{32}{3}$), once the vacuum scales $(\mathcal{V}_0, m_\psi^2)$ are given under $\mathcal{V}'_{\mathrm{spec}}(0)=0$, $\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{spec}}(\psi, \mathcal{A})$ is completely independent of the spectral cutoff regulator ($\partial_{\mu_k}\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{reg}} \equiv 0$) and decomposes uniquely into the shifted basis polynomials of Eq.~\eqref{eq:c4_shifted_potential}, while Cauchy's semigroup functional equation (\catH) uniquely characterizes $\psi_t(\mathcal{A}) = \psi_0 e^{-\lambda_A\mathcal{A}^2}$ (\catD) and Radon--Plancherel convolution establishes the $\mathcal{C}^3$ contact smoothness of $f(\lambda)$ with 4th-derivative jump $\Delta f_{\mathrm{mut}}^{(4)}(1) = -30$ \catD.
  \item \textbf{Lovelock's Theorem (1971) and Gilkey--DeWitt $a_2$ Polynomial (Theorem~\ref{thm:c4_gb_seeley_dewitt_identities}):} Equations~\eqref{eq:c4_gb_split}--\eqref{eq:c4_gb_volume_divergence} prove Lovelock's Hessian cancellation ($c_{\mathrm{GB}} = 8$) in $\mathcal{G}_5$ \cite{Lovelock1971,Gilkey1995} \catD.
\end{enumerate}
\end{remark}
